- `rubric_version`: `chapter_6b_v3`
- `calibration_batch_id` and `calibrated_against`
- `score_1_to_10`, or null with `insufficient_evidence_reason`
- `confidence_score` and `plausible_score_range`
- `decisive_positive_evidence` and `decisive_negative_evidence`, referencing evidence items
- `inherited_baseline_and_constraints` and `ruler_attribution`
- `supported_lenses` and `missing_or_weak_lenses`
- `contrary_evidence` and unresolved disagreements
- `source_mix` and `structured_prior_summary`
- `chapter_rationale`, `lower_anchor_rejected`, and `higher_anchor_rejected`
- `manual_review_required`, `manual_review_reason_type`, and `manual_review_reason`

Chapter-specific extras: `evidenced_service_domains`, `distribution_and_dignity`,
`crisis_context`, `institutional_durability`, and `trajectory`.

`Insufficient_evidence` is a permitted judgment for an exceptionally thin case, not an automatic consequence of missing lenses. Natural-language descriptions are preferred to false enum precision.

## Calibration, Confidence, and Bias Checks

Check national-average versus group/region bias, income and baseline comparability, demographic lag, donor/subnational attribution, conflict/disaster/pandemic context, open-versus-closed data quality, official-statistics manipulation, survivorship, measurement breaks, population scale, present-day-standard/hindsight bias and visibility bias. Compare feasible improvement as well as absolute levels; do not excuse deliberate exclusion because a country is poor.

## Smoke-Test Cases

| Ruler | Country | Period | Calibration purpose |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Park Chung-hee | South Korea | 1961–1979 | rapid gains, low baseline and authoritarian distribution caveats |
| Lee Kuan Yew | Singapore | 1965–1990 | housing/health/education and durable-state high comparison |
| Lula da Silva | Brazil | 2003–2010 | social protection, food security and inequality |
| Ellen Johnson Sirleaf | Liberia | 2006–2018 | post-conflict rebuilding, donor role and Ebola shock |
| Paul Kagame | Rwanda | 2000–2023 | health gains with data openness and rights/distribution checks |
| Narendra Modi | India | 2014–2023 | sanitation/protection scale versus quality and uneven access |
| Hugo Chávez | Venezuela | 1999–2013 | early access gains versus politicization and durability |
| Nicolae Ceaușescu | Romania | 1981–1989 | austerity-driven deprivation low anchor |
| Kim Jong Il | North Korea | 1994–2011 | famine, state responsibility and closed-information uncertainty |
| Jacinda Ardern | New Zealand | 2020–2022 | high baseline, pandemic response and inequality test |
| Sirimavo Bandaranaike | Sri Lanka | 1960–1965 | less-visible historical, food/education policy and gender comparison |

## Acceptance Checklist

- A numeric score requires a ruler-attributed social-welfare action and some evidence
  of delivery, implementation, remedy, or attributable outcome. A budget announcement
  or inherited human-development level alone is insufficient.

- The exact ten lenses remain visible and produce one chapter score only.
- Evidence is local-first, cited, source-profiled and reusable across real lens relationships.
- Baseline, shocks, ruler authority, implementation and outcomes are separated.
- Access, quality, affordability, distribution, dignity and durability are considered without rigid domain gates.
- Missing lenses/domains reduce confidence and never invalidate or count as failure.
- One judge calibrates all rulers with the same chapter rubric.
- Flexible LLM wording and incomplete mappings remain acceptable.
- Bias checks and adjacent-anchor rejections are case-specific.
- Smoke cases are judged together before activation.


## docs/methodology/chapter-guides/7b-integrity.md

# 7B. Ruler Personal Integrity and Honesty

Status: draft; revised after the 2024 personal-nexus audit; requires v4 rejudgment; v1 invalid

## Chapter Identity

- Chapter: `7B`
- Category: `ruler_personal_integrity_and_honesty`
- Evidence strategy: one ruler-period dossier, researched across ten overlapping lenses
- Judge strategy: one chapter judge applies one comparative meter across all eligible rulers
- Rubric version: `chapter_7b_v4`

## Chapter Purpose

Assess whether the ruler uses public office honestly and for public rather than
personal, family, business, factional, or patronage advantage. The chapter joins
truthfulness, correction, conflicts of interest, self-enrichment, appointments,
independent scrutiny, concealment, commitments, favoritism, and ethical example
into one final 1-10 integrity score.

The ten questions are evidence lenses, not ten equally weighted sub-scores.
Evidence may illuminate several lenses, and missing evidence for one lens lowers
confidence rather than invalidating the dossier or preventing a chapter score.

## Ten Evidence Lenses

The question text below is authoritative and must not be paraphrased in the
research manifest.

| ID | Evidence lens |
|---|---|
| **7B.1** | Does the ruler habitually tell the truth to the public, legislature, courts, allies, and international partners, especially on matters where deception would protect power or reputation? |
| **7B.2** | Does the ruler admit errors, correct false claims, and allow truthful reporting, or do they knowingly mislead, double down, blame others, and punish truth-tellers? |
| **7B.3** | Does the ruler separate personal/family/business interests from state decisions, public contracts, licensing, regulation, law enforcement, and foreign policy? |
| **7B.4** | Does the ruler or close family profit from office through assets, contracts, monopolies, gifts, bribes, emoluments, insider access, opaque foundations, or hidden conflicts of interest? |
| **7B.5** | Does the ruler appoint competent professionals, or fill government with family, friends, cronies, donors, business associates, loyalists, and yes-men to protect personal power or self-dealing? |
| **7B.6** | Does the ruler tolerate independent investigation of their conduct, assets, campaign finance, conflicts of interest, associates, and concealed official decisions? |
| **7B.7** | Does the ruler use state power to conceal illegal, destructive, or self-serving activity, protect themselves from accountability, punish investigators, or neutralize courts, prosecutors, auditors, media, and whistleblowers? |
| **7B.8** | Does the ruler keep promises and respect formal commitments, or opportunistically reverse positions, manipulate public information, and conceal tradeoffs for personal advantage? |
| **7B.9** | Does the ruler avoid nepotism, favoritism, clientelism, and transactional politics in appointments, pardons, procurement, enforcement, and access to public information? |
| **7B.10** | Does the ruler model ethical standards that improve public trust, or normalize deliberate lying, impunity, self-dealing, conflicts of interest, and cynicism? |

## Scope and Non-Goals

- Judge conduct attributable to the ruler, including conduct knowingly directed,
  enabled, protected, or tolerated by the ruler's close governing network.
- Do not infer personal dishonesty from a country's inherited corruption level.
- Do not equate policy disagreement, honest error, changed policy after new
  evidence, rhetoric, secrecy justified by genuine security/privacy/diplomatic
  needs, or an unproved allegation with deception or corruption.
- Do not reward polished communications, formal ethics rules, prosecutions of
  rivals, or selective transparency without evidence of genuine practice.
- Do not score general competence, prosperity, freedom, or popularity except
  where the facts directly demonstrate integrity or abuse of office.
- Never use the client matrix as evidence.

Country corruption indicators, authoritarian institutions, censorship, detention,
human-rights abuse, and political-freedom conditions are context only unless the
evidence establishes a direct nexus to ruler-specific deception, personal or network
benefit, favoritism, concealment, obstruction of scrutiny, retaliation against an
integrity investigation, or deliberate abuse of office. Lenses 7B.7 and 7B.10 do not
convert every repressive act into personal dishonesty. Score those acts primarily in
3B/4B unless that integrity nexus is evidenced.

Apply this as an item-level admissibility gate, not merely a caveat after scoring. For
each potentially decisive item, identify the ruler's personal statement, interest,
benefit, appointment, protected network, response to scrutiny, concealment, correction,
obstruction, or knowing tolerance after notice. If none is shown, the item receives zero
scoring weight even when it proves authoritarianism, repression, country corruption, or
weak institutions. Do not cite a zero-weight item as decisive or use it to reject an
adjacent anchor.

## Researcher Workflow and Evidence Themes

Follow the local-first researcher guide and source-confidence registry. Query the
exact ruler/country/year or bounded period locally before web discovery. Treat
WGI, V-Dem, and CPI as country-level context and triangulation, not proof of the
ruler's personal conduct.

Research by themes that cut across lenses, not by ten isolated searches:

1. Material public statements, corrections, sworn evidence, and documented
   concealment, including examples of costly candor and adverse findings.
2. Assets, beneficial ownership, gifts, business/family interests, procurement,
   licensing, regulation, pardons, campaign finance, and foreign-policy benefits.
3. Appointments and access patterns: competence, kinship, donor/client ties,
   loyalty demands, and whether appointees facilitate self-dealing or impunity.
4. Courts, prosecutors, auditors, legislatures, ethics bodies, inquiries,
   journalists, and whistleblowers: independence, obstruction, retaliation,
   remedies, and ruler response.
5. Promise and commitment records, ethical rules, institutional reforms, and
   whether practice strengthened or normalized trust and accountability.

Prefer primary legal and financial records, judgments, audit/inquiry findings,
legislative records, asset declarations, procurement records, official
transcripts, sanctions findings with stated evidence, reputable investigative
reporting, and serious academic histories. Official sources are strong for the
fact that a statement, appointment, filing, or rule exists, but conditional for
self-serving claims of honesty. Allegations must remain allegations; seek an
independent finding, underlying record, response, and contrary evidence.

Each accepted item should retain its citation, date, claim, period fit, source
type/confidence, ruler-attribution assessment, and uncertainty. Reuse a stable
evidence item across every relevant lens. Coverage descriptions may use natural
language; no exact status vocabulary is required.

## Common Evidence-Item Semantics

Each material evidence item should carry a stable evidence ID; a concise factual
claim; source URL, title, publisher, and publication/access date where available;
source type and confidence with a short reason; a quotation or precise locator
when available; target-year/ruler-period fit; ruler attribution and
inherited-baseline relevance; its positive, negative, mixed, contextual, or
contrary role; and the supported lens IDs. Fields may be normalized later and
natural-language equivalents are acceptable. The substantive minimum is a
traceable source, intelligible claim, temporal fit, attribution, and evidence
role. One item may support multiple lenses without duplication.

## Attribution, Sparse Evidence, and Historical Cases

Separate personal direction or tolerance from inherited institutions and acts of
family, ministers, parties, businesses, or security services. Consider formal and
informal authority, time in office, credible knowledge, ability to intervene,
action after notice, coalition constraints, and whether the ruler benefited.
Close-associate misconduct is not automatically the ruler's, but repeated benefit,
protection, appointment, obstruction, or failure to act after credible notice can
support attribution.

For older, obscure, closed, short-tenure, or poorly archived cases, use the best
available contemporary records and reputable later scholarship. Do not interpret
silence as integrity where reporting and accountability were suppressed. A dossier
with only a few strong themes can still be judged; narrow coverage, one-sided
archives, weak attribution, or missing adverse-case searches should reduce
confidence and may widen the defensible score range. Do not manufacture lens
coverage or impose a modern disclosure regime anachronistically.

Silence, favorable institutions, or the absence of reported allegations cannot prove
integrity. Scores above 5 require affirmative ruler-specific evidence of candor,
separation of interests, correction, professional appointments, or acceptance of
genuine scrutiny. Scores below 4 require ruler-specific recurring deception,
self-dealing, favoritism, patronage, concealment, or obstruction. If neither side is
supported by traceable ruler-specific conduct, return null rather than rewarding or
punishing the information environment.

### Absolute-scale and visibility guardrails

The annual cohort does not define the endpoints. Use fixed cross-period reference
patterns rather than assigning the most opaque current ruler to the floor:

- **1:** Personal or family extraction, deliberate deception, cronyism, and
  state-backed concealment or impunity are central instruments of rule, supported by
  direct findings or strong convergent ruler-specific evidence. Mobutu-era kleptocracy
  or Marcos-family extraction are reference patterns; generic authoritarian control is
  insufficient.
- **2–3:** Severe or recurring ruler-specific deception, conflicts, favoritism,
  self-dealing, or obstruction spans important domains, without reaching that floor.
- **4–5:** Genuinely mixed personal conduct: attributable safeguards or honest conduct
  coexist with material lapses, patronage, conflicts, broken commitments, or weak
  accountability.
- **6–7:** Generally honest public service with real scrutiny and correction, but
  notable attributable lapses or opacity remain.
- **8–9:** Sustained candor, separation of interests, professional appointments, and
  acceptance of independent scrutiny, including evidence under adverse incentives.
- **10:** Exceptional costly candor and durable strengthening against self-dealing and
  impunity under strong contrary incentives.

Normalize visibility before scoring. Numerous fact checks, press controversies, or
allegations in an open system do not establish a worse pattern than silence in a closed
system. Conversely, closed-system silence does not establish integrity or personal
corruption. Compare materiality, repetition, findings, personal benefit, response, and
remedy—not article counts. When admissible personal-nexus evidence is thin on both
sides, return null rather than converting repression into a low score or silence into a
midpoint.

## Holistic 1-10 Chapter Rubric

Synthesize the ruler's pattern, materiality, intent, personal benefit, use of state
power, duration, corrective behavior, and institutional effect. Severe direct
self-dealing or obstruction may properly dominate several weaker positive lenses;
conversely, an isolated error should not outweigh a broad record of candor and
accountability. Do not average lens labels or count citations.

| Score | Chapter anchor |
|---:|---|
| 1 | Deliberate deception, personal or family extraction, cronyism, and state-backed concealment/impunity are central instruments of rule and materially corrupt public office. |
| 2-3 | Severe or recurring dishonesty, conflicts, self-dealing, favoritism, or obstruction span important domains; independent accountability is attacked or largely ineffective. |
| 4-5 | A material mixed record: meaningful integrity safeguards or honest conduct coexist with recurring deception, patronage, conflicts, broken commitments, or weak accountability. |
| 6-7 | Generally honest and public-serving conduct with real scrutiny and correction, but notable lapses, opacity, favoritism, conflicts, or accountability weaknesses remain. |
| 8-9 | Strong, sustained truthfulness, separation of interests, professional public service, openness to scrutiny, correction, and ethical example; credible lapses are rare and remedied. |
| 10 | Exceptional and durable integrity under adverse incentives: costly candor, exemplary separation of interests, robust independent accountability, and institutions strengthened against self-dealing and impunity. |

## Confidence

Report confidence separately on a 0-100 scale. Base it on source authority and
independence, ruler attribution, temporal fit, information environment, diversity
of themes and source types, contrary-evidence search, and agreement. Evidence
volume alone is not confidence. Missing lenses normally lower confidence; they do
not mechanically lower the chapter score. State the most plausible score range
when uncertainty is material.

## One-Judge Comparative Workflow

One judge owns 7B for the target year/period across all eligible rulers:

1. Read every dossier's 7B evidence and explicit gaps without demanding uniform
   coverage terminology or perfect mapping tables.
2. Separate identity-quarantined cases; retain sparse cases with reduced
   confidence rather than discarding them.
3. Establish provisional low, middle, and high batch anchors using diverse
   regimes, periods, visibility levels, and evidence environments.
4. Make tentative holistic scores, then compare neighboring cases for consistent
   thresholds of intent, materiality, personal benefit, obstruction, correction,
   and attribution.
5. Revisit outliers and cases whose score rests mainly on country indicators,
   official self-report, accusation volume, or media visibility.
6. Emit one final chapter score, confidence/range, rationale, strongest positive
   and negative evidence, missing themes, contrary evidence, and review flag.

## Common Chapter-Judge Output Envelope

The judge returns one object per ruler using these exact semantic field names so
all eight chapter outputs can be normalized by the same inexpensive formatter:

- `chapter_id`: `7B`
- `rubric_version`: `chapter_7b_v4`
- `calibration_batch_id` and `calibrated_against`
- `score_1_to_10`, or null with `insufficient_evidence_reason`
- `confidence_score` and `plausible_score_range`
- `decisive_positive_evidence` and `decisive_negative_evidence`, referencing
  stable evidence IDs
- `inherited_baseline_and_constraints` and `ruler_attribution`
- `supported_lenses` and `missing_or_weak_lenses`
- `contrary_evidence`
- `source_mix` and `structured_prior_summary`
- `chapter_rationale`, `lower_anchor_rejected`, and `higher_anchor_rejected`
- `manual_review_required`, `manual_review_reason_type`, and `manual_review_reason`

Harmless serialization differences can be repaired after judgment, but the
semantic envelope must be complete. A null score is reserved for evidence too
thin or untraceable to support any responsible range, unresolved identity, or
attribution too uncertain to judge—not merely missing lenses or flexible coverage
wording.

## Chapter-Specific Calibration

Use the common bias checks in `cited-evaluation-calibration.md`, interpreted at
chapter level, plus:

- `rubric_version`: `chapter_7b_v4`
- `integrity_pattern`: `systemic_abuse`, `recurring_serious_abuse`, `mixed`,
  `generally_high_integrity`, `exceptional_integrity`, or `unclear`
- `deception_pattern`: `strategic_systematic`, `recurring_material`, `mixed`,
  `limited`, `not_shown`, or `unclear`
- `personal_benefit_evidence`: `direct`, `strong_inference`, `limited`,
  `not_shown`, or `unclear`
- `accountability_response`: `obstructed_or_retalitated`, `impunity`, `partial`,
  `accepted_and_corrected`, `strengthened`, or `unclear`
- `conflict_and_patronage_pattern`: `systemic`, `recurring`, `mixed`, `limited`,
  `not_shown`, or `unclear`
- `ruler_attribution`: concise assessment of direction, knowledge, benefit,
  protection/tolerance, authority, and action after notice
- `lens_strengths` and `lens_gaps`: concise ID lists with natural-language notes
- `defensible_score_range`: lower and upper score under current evidence

Explicitly check visibility/fact-checking bias, silence under repression,
allegation-versus-finding, family/associate attribution, repetition of one scandal,
anachronistic disclosure expectations, and whether national corruption conditions
were wrongly personalized.

## Smoke-Test Set

These are calibration roles, not predetermined scores.

| Ruler | Country | Period | Calibration purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| Richard Nixon | United States | 1972-1974 | documented deception, concealment, institutions, and remedy |
| Donald Trump | United States | 2017-2020 | high visibility, business conflicts, appointments, and fact-check volume |
| Nelson Mandela | South Africa | 1994-1999 | high-integrity candidate requiring genuine adverse search |
| Lee Kuan Yew | Singapore | 1965-1990 | low-corruption governance with constrained scrutiny |
| Ferdinand Marcos | Philippines | 1972-1986 | family enrichment, patronage, authoritarian archive |
| Angela Merkel | Germany | 2005-2021 | long-tenure, high-anchor candidate and policy-change boundary |
| Vladimir Putin | Russia | 2000-2022 | closed environment, associates, concealment, and attribution |
| José Mujica | Uruguay | 2010-2015 | modest-lifestyle reputation versus institutional evidence |
| Mobutu Sese Seko | Zaire | 1965-1997 | personal rule and extraction in a sparse historical record |
| Jacinda Ardern | New Zealand | 2020-2022 | crisis candor, correction, and high-visibility scrutiny |
| Ellen Johnson Sirleaf | Liberia | 2006-2018 | post-conflict institutions, family allegations, and lower-visibility evidence |
| Indira Gandhi | India | 1966-1977 | historical woman ruler, emergency accountability, and non-Western archives |
| Maria Liberia-Peters | Netherlands Antilles | 1984-1986 | lower-visibility woman ruler and sparse-evidence confidence discipline |

## Acceptance Checklist

- Every numeric score requires direct ruler-specific integrity evidence: a personal
  act, disclosure, conflict response, truthfulness episode, accountability choice, or
  comparably attributable pattern. Generic repression, institutional strength, and
  absence of reported scandal are context only. Without the personal nexus, return
  null rather than `5`; evidence explicitly described as absent cannot support above
  `5`.

- All ten authoritative lenses are present verbatim and treated as overlapping,
  non-equal evidence lenses.
- Local-first order, source-confidence fields, citations, and run profile are
  retained.
- Evidence items satisfy the common traceability, temporal-fit, attribution,
  evidence-role, and lens-link semantics.
- Ruler conduct is separated from inherited national corruption and associate
  conduct is attributed explicitly.
- Allegations, findings, honest error, secrecy, deception, correction, benefit,
  and obstruction are distinguished.
- Missing lenses reduce confidence rather than invalidate or mechanically depress
  the score.
- One judge compares all rulers with one holistic rubric and records adjacent-case
  calibration.
- Final output uses the common chapter-judge envelope, including adjacent-anchor
  comparisons and an explicit manual-review flag.
- Smoke testing and revision occur before production activation.


## docs/methodology/chapter-guides/8b-effectiveness.md

# 8B. Ruler Effectiveness and Competence

Status: draft; revised after 2020 v2 acceptance audit; requires v3 rejudgment

## Chapter Identity

- Chapter: `8B`
- Category: `ruler_effectiveness_and_competence`
- Evidence strategy: one ruler-period dossier, researched across ten overlapping lenses
- Judge strategy: one chapter judge applies one comparative meter across all eligible rulers
- Rubric version: `chapter_8b_v3`

## Chapter Purpose

Assess how effectively the ruler defines a governing program, converts it into
state or ruling-network action, coordinates implementation, adapts, and advances
their own stated or reliably revealed objectives. The ten questions are evidence
lenses feeding one final 1-10 chapter score, not equal sub-scores.

This chapter is ideology-neutral. Moral worth and execution competence are
separate. A ruler may execute a cruel, aggressive, corrupt, or authoritarian
program competently and score highly in 8B while scoring very poorly in other
chapters. Never smuggle moral approval or condemnation into the competence score.

## Ten Evidence Lenses

The question text below is authoritative and must not be paraphrased in the
research manifest.

| ID | Evidence lens |
|---|---|
| **8B.1** | Does the ruler articulate a clear governing ideology, strategic direction, or program, including explicit or revealed goals for power, policy, or regime control, that can be evaluated against later action? |
| **8B.2** | Does the ruler translate that program into concrete priorities, plans, budgets, appointments, timelines, institutions, and enforcement mechanisms? |
| **8B.3** | Does the ruler mobilize the state apparatus, party, military, bureaucracy, coalition, or ruling network effectively toward the chosen program and the ruler's own goals? |
| **8B.4** | Does the ruler select and empower people who are capable of executing the program, whether professionals, loyal operators, technocrats, organizers, security officials, or coercive administrators? |
| **8B.5** | Does the ruler maintain internal discipline, coordination, control, and follow-through across ministries, regions, territory, institutions, security forces, party structures, and implementing agencies? |
| **8B.6** | Does the ruler convert declarations into observable implementation and state reach rather than leaving goals as slogans, speeches, symbolic gestures, or propaganda only? |
| **8B.7** | Do outcome indicators move in the direction the ruler claimed or revealed they sought, after allowing for realistic lags, inherited conditions, and external constraints? |
| **8B.8** | When tactics fail, does the ruler adapt methods, replace ineffective implementers, reallocate resources, or otherwise correct course to keep advancing the program and maintaining effective control? |
| **8B.9** | Does the ruler manage crises, opposition, international relationships, and institutional resistance in a way that preserves or advances the regime's chosen objectives, durability, and influence, regardless of whether those objectives are morally good? |
| **8B.10** | By the end of the relevant period, is the ruler closer to achieving the stated or revealed ideological, policy, power-consolidation, or international-influence program than at the start, accounting for short-term wins, long-term durability, inherited conditions, and external shocks? |

## Scope and Non-Goals

- Establish an explicit or reliably revealed program before judging execution.
  Repeated discriminating choices may reveal goals; hindsight speculation may not.
- Evaluate strategic clarity, operationalization, mobilization, personnel fit,
  coordination, implementation, outcomes, adaptation, crisis management, and
  durable progress as mutually informing evidence.
- Do not judge whether goals are democratic, peaceful, lawful, prosperous, humane,
  or personally honest. Those judgments belong in 1B-7B.
- Do not reward repression, propaganda, longevity, survival, economic growth,
  administrative size, or institutional quality by itself. Ask whether it
  effectively served the ruler's identified program.
- Do not assume failure from an outcome the ruler did not seek, except where
  maintaining basic government function was necessarily part of governing.
- Do not use the client matrix as evidence.

Before reading outcomes or assigning a score, freeze a portfolio of the ruler's major
stated or reliably revealed programs plus necessary core-government functions. Apply
the same breadth rule across regime types. Do not cherry-pick the easiest achieved
goal, reduce a democratic government to its broadest welfare promises, or reduce an
authoritarian government to survival, weapons, or power consolidation. Survival and
consolidation may be one material program component, never the entire portfolio by
default.

## Researcher Workflow and Evidence Themes

Follow the local-first researcher guide and source-confidence registry. Query the
exact case locally. WGI, V-Dem, BTI, budgets, and outcome indicators are useful
context or corroboration, but generic state capacity is not ruler effectiveness.

Research chronologically across themes rather than issuing one search per lens:

1. Identify stated programs from platforms, manifestos, coalition agreements,
   speeches, strategies, directives, budgets, and plans. Identify revealed goals
   only from repeated choices that distinguish them from rhetoric or speculation.
2. Trace conversion into priorities, resources, laws, institutions, appointments,
   timelines, enforcement, and measurable milestones.
3. Examine whether the state, party, military, coalition, bureaucracy, territorial
   network, or informal machinery actually mobilized, coordinated, and followed
   through; include bottlenecks and implementation gaps.
4. Assess whether personnel were capable for the assigned program. Ideological or
   coercive operators may be effective implementers in this narrow chapter even
   when their conduct is reprehensible elsewhere.
5. Compare promised/revealed objectives with implementation and outcomes after
   realistic lags, inherited trends, peer conditions, shocks, and resources.
6. Trace learning, replacement, reallocation, crisis response, resistance
   management, short-term gains, reversals, durability, and end-of-period position.

Prefer primary program and budget documents, legislation, administrative and
audit records, implementation data, credible outcome series, contemporaneous
records, serious academic histories, independent evaluations, and reputable
reporting. Official sources establish declared goals and formal actions but not
successful execution by themselves. Preserve failures, contradictions, abandoned
priorities, propaganda-only claims, alternative causal explanations, and adverse
side effects; record the latter for cross-chapter use without morally deducting
them in 8B.

Reuse each stable evidence item across relevant lenses. Natural-language coverage
and gaps are acceptable; publication must not depend on exact status terms,
sequential IDs, or exhaustive mapping syntax.

## Common Evidence-Item Semantics

Each material evidence item should carry a stable evidence ID; a concise factual
claim; source URL, title, publisher, and publication/access date where available;
source type and confidence with a short reason; a quotation or precise locator
when available; target-year/ruler-period fit; ruler attribution and
inherited-baseline relevance; its positive, negative, mixed, contextual, or
contrary role; and the supported lens IDs. Fields may be normalized later and
natural-language equivalents are acceptable. The substantive minimum is a
traceable source, intelligible claim, temporal fit, attribution, and evidence
role. One item may support multiple lenses without duplication.

## Attribution, Sparse Evidence, and Historical Cases

Separate ruler capability from inherited state capacity, predecessor programs,
subordinate initiative, favorable trends, external patrons, and luck. Assess
formal/informal control, tenure, coalition and institutional constraints,
resources, shocks, lag, ownership of appointments and priorities, response to
implementation information, and plausible counterfactual trajectory.

For obscure or historical rulers, later scholarship, archives, diplomatic
records, budgets, institutional histories, and observable implementation may be
more informative than missing modern indicators. Oral or informal programs can
be evidenced by repeated choices. Sparse evidence should reduce confidence and
widen the defensible range, not force a neutral score or invalidate the case.
Short tenure may permit judgment of direction and mobilization while leaving
outcomes unresolved. Closed systems may document plans abundantly while hiding
failures; official volume is not proof of execution.

## Holistic 1-10 Chapter Rubric

Synthesize the causal chain from program through action to durable progress.
Lenses are redundant and qualitatively weighted: clear plans with no execution
cannot score highly; favorable outcomes without ruler-attributable direction or
action warrant limited credit; a major strategic collapse can outweigh several
administrative successes. Never calculate a simple mean or score by citation
count.

Strategic clarity, centralized control, personnel discipline, and planning without
independently evidenced implementation and goal progress cannot exceed 5. Scores above
5 require attributable implementation plus progress across material parts of the
frozen portfolio. Scores above 7 additionally require independent evidence of outcomes,
durability, and adaptation rather than official architecture, coercion, longevity, or
survival alone. Harmful programs remain morally neutral here, but they must still show
actual advancement rather than mere capacity to command.

| Score | Chapter anchor |
|---:|---|
| 1 | No coherent program or effective governing control, or catastrophic inability to mobilize and execute: recurring chaos, paralysis, collapse, or decisive movement away from the ruler's own objectives. |
| 2-3 | Weak or unstable direction and serious operational failure; plans remain largely symbolic, coordination repeatedly breaks down, adaptation is poor, and major objectives substantially recede. |
| 4-5 | Mixed competence: some clear priorities and real delivery coexist with major implementation gaps, inconsistent control, failed initiatives, weak adaptation, or fragile/temporary results. |
| 6-7 | Generally effective direction and execution across important priorities, with meaningful mobilization and progress but notable delays, failures, constraints, reversals, or durability concerns. |
| 8-9 | Strong strategic-operational alignment, capable personnel and coordination, adaptive implementation, and substantial durable advancement of the ruler's program across major priorities. |
| 10 | Exceptional, sustained mastery of direction, mobilization, execution, adaptation, crisis/resistance management, and durable goal realization under demanding conditions. |

These anchors apply equally to benevolent and harmful programs. The rationale
must separately name the program and state that moral effects are evaluated in
other chapters.

## Confidence

Report confidence separately on a 0-100 scale. Consider program identification,
ruler attribution, causal trace from inputs to implementation/outcomes, source
independence, time fit and lag, external-shock controls, source/theme diversity,
closed-system opacity, and contrary explanations. Missing lenses lower confidence
rather than automatically lowering the chapter score. Give a defensible score
range where evidence or attribution is materially uncertain.

## One-Judge Comparative Workflow

One judge owns 8B across all eligible rulers for the target year/period:

1. Read every 8B dossier, identify each ruler's explicit or reliably revealed
   program, and quarantine identity failures—not sparse evidence.
2. Separate moral evaluation from execution before scoring. Record cross-chapter
   harms without deducting them here.
3. Establish batch anchors spanning democratic and authoritarian, beneficial and
   harmful, modern and historical, high- and low-capacity contexts.
4. Assign tentative holistic scores along the program-to-results chain, then
   compare neighbors for consistent treatment of inherited capacity, resources,
   shocks, tenure, lag, luck, resistance, and durability.
5. Revisit cases dominated by generic governance indicators, official claims,
   survival/tenure, favorable outcomes without attribution, or moral halo/horns.
6. Emit one chapter score, confidence/range, identified program, rationale,
   strongest execution and failure evidence, unresolved causal alternatives,
   missing themes, and review flag.

## Common Chapter-Judge Output Envelope

The judge returns one object per ruler using these exact semantic field names so
all eight chapter outputs can be normalized by the same inexpensive formatter:

- `chapter_id`: `8B`
- `rubric_version`: `chapter_8b_v3`
- `calibration_batch_id` and `calibrated_against`
- `score_1_to_10`, or null with `insufficient_evidence_reason`
- `confidence_score` and `plausible_score_range`
- `decisive_positive_evidence` and `decisive_negative_evidence`, referencing
  stable evidence IDs
- `inherited_baseline_and_constraints` and `ruler_attribution`
- `supported_lenses` and `missing_or_weak_lenses`
- `contrary_evidence`
- `source_mix` and `structured_prior_summary`
- `chapter_rationale`, `lower_anchor_rejected`, and `higher_anchor_rejected`
- `manual_review_required`, `manual_review_reason_type`, and `manual_review_reason`

Harmless serialization differences can be repaired after judgment, but the
semantic envelope must be complete. A null score is reserved for evidence too
thin or untraceable to support any responsible range, unresolved identity, or
attribution too uncertain to judge—not merely missing lenses or flexible coverage
wording. The rationale must identify the program and state explicitly that its
moral worth did not affect the execution score.

## Chapter-Specific Calibration

Use common calibration and bias checks where meaningful, plus:

- `rubric_version`: `chapter_8b_v3`
- `program_basis`: `explicit`, `mostly_explicit`, `mixed`, `mostly_revealed`,
  `speculative`, or `unclear`
- `program_type`: list from `policy`, `state_building`, `regime_control`,
  `power_consolidation`, `international_influence`, `survival`, or `other`
- `strategic_clarity`: `none`, `weak`, `partial`, `clear`, `exceptional`, or `unclear`
- `operationalization`: `absent`, `weak`, `mixed`, `strong`, `exceptional`, or `unclear`
- `mobilization_and_coordination`: `failed`, `weak`, `mixed`, `strong`,
  `exceptional`, or `unclear`
- `adaptation`: `counterproductive`, `rigid`, `mixed`, `effective`,
  `exceptional`, `not_observed`, or `unclear`
- `goal_progress`: `major_reversal`, `net_regression`, `mixed`, `substantial`,
  `exceptional`, or `too_early_or_unclear`
- `durability`: `collapsed`, `fragile`, `mixed`, `durable`, `highly_durable`,
  `too_early`, or `unclear`
- `ruler_attribution`: concise assessment of ownership, authority, inherited
  capacity, subordinate action, shocks, luck, and alternative causes
- `morality_separation_check`: explicit statement that approval/disapproval of
  objectives did not alter the score
- `lens_strengths`, `lens_gaps`, and `defensible_score_range`

Explicitly check state-capacity inheritance, survivor bias, outcome/luck bias,
propaganda and closed-system opacity, documentation advantages, hindsight-defined
goals, short-tenure/lag bias, moral halo/horns, and whether coercion was mistaken
for competence without evidence of goal advancement.

## Smoke-Test Set

These are calibration roles, not predetermined scores.

| Ruler | Country | Period | Calibration purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lee Kuan Yew | Singapore | 1965-1990 | long-horizon direction, state capacity, durability |
| Deng Xiaoping | China | 1978-1992 | revealed/pragmatic program and adaptation |
| Adolf Hitler | Germany | 1933-1945 | harmful program, early execution, strategic catastrophe |
| Joseph Stalin | Soviet Union | 1928-1953 | coercive implementation and moral-separation stress test |
| Margaret Thatcher | United Kingdom | 1979-1990 | explicit democratic program, resistance, and durable change |
| Mikhail Gorbachev | Soviet Union | 1985-1991 | adaptation, partial achievement, loss of control, disputed goals |
| Boris Yeltsin | Russia | 1991-1999 | crisis, weak coordination, inherited collapse, mixed reforms |
| Paul Kagame | Rwanda | 2000-2020 | development and regime-control goals with attribution concerns |
| Volodymyr Zelenskyy | Ukraine | 2019-2023 | program transformed by extreme external shock |
| Liz Truss | United Kingdom | 2022 | short tenure, explicit program, rapid implementation failure |
| Franklin D. Roosevelt | United States | 1933-1945 | institutional mobilization across depression and war |
| Mobutu Sese Seko | Zaire | 1965-1997 | survival/personal-rule effectiveness versus state deterioration |
| Ellen Johnson Sirleaf | Liberia | 2006-2018 | post-conflict rebuilding, donor attribution, and woman ruler |
| Sirimavo Bandaranaike | Sri Lanka | 1960-1965 | historical woman ruler, coalition/state capacity, and non-Western evidence |
| Sylvanus Olympio | Togo | 1960-1963 | short, lower-visibility postcolonial program and sparse archives |

## Acceptance Checklist

- Missing implementation evidence never establishes ineffectiveness; it produces a
  null when no ruler-attributed execution fact remains. Scores above `5` require
  observable implementation or coordination, and scores above `7` additionally
  require independent evidence of attributable outcomes, adaptation, or durable
  progress across more than one material program component.

- All ten authoritative lenses are present verbatim and serve one holistic score.
- Ideology-neutrality is explicit and moral worth is separated from execution in
  research, scoring, calibration, and rationale.
- An explicit or reliably revealed program is established before execution is
  judged; hindsight speculation is labeled and constrained.
- Local-first order, citations, source profiling, and run profile are retained.
- Evidence items satisfy the common traceability, temporal-fit, attribution,
  evidence-role, and lens-link semantics.
- Ruler action is separated from inherited capacity, subordinates, luck, trends,
  shocks, tenure, and realistic outcome lags.
- Missing lenses reduce confidence rather than invalidate or mechanically depress
  the score.
- One judge compares all rulers using one meter; lenses are neither equally
  weighted nor mechanically averaged.
- Final output uses the common chapter-judge envelope, including adjacent-anchor
  comparisons and an explicit manual-review flag.
- Smoke testing and revision occur before production activation.


Your task is research, not final formatting. Start with the deduplicated local
package before inspecting internet discovery. Establish a chapter-by-chapter local
baseline: which facts exist, their exact years/sources/warnings, what they measure,
what is only country or inherited-capacity context, and which attribution or narrative
gaps require internet evidence. Do not re-fetch a structured dataset already supplied
locally. Absence of a local row is a gap, not proof that the real-world condition was
zero, peaceful, safe, responsible, or inapplicable. The package's
    `candidate_methodology_ids` are routing hints, not proof that a fact directly answers
    every listed lens; decide and explain actual reuse yourself.
    For oversized time series, the inlined package contains all target-year facts,
    audited longitudinal signals, and boundary observations rather than every annual row.
    `facts_omitted` records the bounded reduction. Do not treat omitted annual rows as
    missing evidence; the parent retains and restores their complete hashed provenance.
Any `error_methodology_ids` entry is a local-input failure, not ordinary missing
evidence: keep it visible as a separate data-quality issue. Continue collecting useful
web evidence for the lens, but do not claim that web material repaired the local
extraction failure. Use the referenced disposition reason and guidance
to distinguish an extraction error, absent country-year, empty mapped fields, and a
genuinely inapplicable lens.

Before lens work, perform one broad ruler-period reconnaissance to understand the
ruler, period, major events, and likely source families across all eight chapters.
As part of reconnaissance, build a cited evidence-environment assessment covering
criticism and civil-society opportunity, censorship/surveillance/intimidation and
self-censorship, safe reporting channels, official-statistics reliability, languages
and archives searched, source-family concentration, repeated coverage of one event,
complaint volume versus reporting freedom, relevant population/exposure denominators,
inherited conditions and shocks, practical ruler authority, and chapter-specific bias
risks. Record the stable provisional IDs supporting these conclusions. Do not score or
apply a regime correction.
During reconnaissance, establish one cited authority baseline covering the ruler's
formal office, responsibility for national policy and appointments, command or party
authority where relevant, and material legal or coalition constraints. Reuse this
baseline across chapters. For Chapters 1B-6B and 8B, formal responsibility can support
attribution without proof of a personal order; Chapter 7B still requires a personal
integrity nexus.
Then work through only the selected chapters and methodology IDs in the job, in guide
order. A full-ruler job selects chapters 1B to 8B and all ten lenses inside each; a
bounded pilot selecting one lens must not silently expand to its other nine chapter
lenses.
This is one continuing ruler research session and one growing evidence library, not
80 independent research tasks. Before every lens,
inspect the accumulated ledger and map any already relevant evidence. Search again
only for the material themes, contrary interpretations, or attribution links that the
current lens still lacks. Append genuinely new evidence and continue; never restart
the ruler research or reproduce an existing fact merely because a new lens can use it.
Do not stop after one broad query. At the start of each chapter, build a lightweight
candidate pool with several differently framed searches: broad topic, event-specific,
named source/archive, adverse or contrary, and local-language searches where useful.
Record query, title, URL, and candidate status without satisfying the evidence schema.
Search tools expose a ranked sample rather than the whole web, so paginate when
supported and vary queries. Then open promising pages and documents, extract exact
claims and locators, and filter them into the evidence ledger. Adjust depth to evidence
abundance: a globally
prominent contemporary ruler normally requires substantially more searching than an
obscure or closed historical case. Record why each lens is covered, partly covered,
explicitly searched without usable evidence, or blocked, and why each chapter is
saturated before moving on.

Record accepted material immediately in `research-materials.md` inside the supplied
output directory before starting another search. Keep one append-only global evidence
ledger for the ruler-period. The ledger may use a Markdown table or one JSON object per
item, but every accepted item must use the same fields and one stable provisional ID.
When file writing is available, also maintain `research-ledger-manifest.json` at the
supplied path as a small accounting index with this shape:
`{"schema_version":"ruler_research_ledger_manifest_v1","entries":[...]}`.
Each entry must contain `provisional_id`, `canonical_fact_key`, `chapter_ids`,
`methodology_ids`, and `disposition`, where `chapter_ids` lists every relevant chapter,
`methodology_ids` lists every exact lens explicitly mapped by the researcher, and
disposition is `final_evidence`, `context`, `discovery_only`, or `rejected`. A rejected
entry must also contain a specific `reason`. Update the manifest whenever the ledger
changes. This is not a second evidence format: it is only the complete key/disposition
index used to prove that formatting did not silently lose notebook material.
Chapter notes and prose may remain flexible. The manifest is an optional accounting aid:
never shorten, distort, or abandon substantive research merely to satisfy it. A complete
permissive notebook remains the authoritative handoff when the model cannot write the file.

For every retained evidence item record:
- a stable provisional ID;
- a canonical fact key formed from the canonical source URL, exact locator, and one
  materially distinct claim, so the same fact is not recreated under another chapter;
- exact approved URL or canonical `local-prior:<methodology_id>` locator from the
  supplied fact record;
- title, publisher, and date when known;
- factual claim and a concise main-points summary;
- a precise source locator: PDF page/table/figure, HTML section plus paragraph, legal
  section, transcript timestamp, dataset row/field, or the supplied exact local-prior
  locator; also retain a short supporting excerpt when available;
- source type/confidence and limitations;
- target-period fit and ruler attribution;
- contrary or mitigating points;
- candidate chapters and lenses it can inform.

If the exact underlying URL or locator is unavailable, label the item
`gateway_only`, `locator_missing`, or `underlying_source_missing` and keep it out of
the defensible evidence count. A homepage, search-result page, document index,
`release page`, `article`, or organization name is not a precise locator. Never guess
a locator. Never bundle multiple URLs or materially distinct claims into one ledger
item merely to save space; create separate items when their provenance differs.

Before adding a new item, check the entire ruler ledger for the same canonical fact
key. Reuse its stable ID and add every genuinely relevant chapter/lens mapping instead
of creating chapter-prefixed copies. Mapping is deliberately many-to-many: do not pick
one "best" lens and hide the evidence from other lenses it directly informs.
Local priors are context unless the fact itself directly measures the ruler's conduct;
they use their supplied stable locator and must not be counted as internet sources.

Aim for 5–20 defensible source-claim units per selected chapter, normally about 10.
This is the filtered ledger size, not a search-results ceiling.
A source-claim unit is one traceable source supporting one materially distinct claim;
a heading, excerpt, paragraph, empty prior, alternate/AMP URL, or repeated statement
is not another item. A long report may support several materially distinct claims when
each has a precise locator; retain them and report source concentration instead of
using a per-URL cap. Reuse one stable item across chapters instead of duplicating it globally.
This is a goal, not a hard gate: never pad, invent, or lower source standards to meet
a count. If a chapter remains below five, inspect every supplied candidate, record
candidate-level rejection reasons, and name the exact missing themes and source types.
Do not declare a chapter ready merely because it reached a count. It is ready when the
concrete record is sufficient to present relevant governing conduct, contrary material,
and attribution fairly; otherwise continue the exact missing themes or document a
credible blocker.

For each chapter, record discovery queries, candidate outcomes, and suggested lens
links for accepted evidence. Do not spend research time producing 80 final coverage
rows; the formatter derives exact coverage from explicit mappings. If a theme remains
empty, first run a lens-specific source-landscape pass phrased in the plain language of
the lens. Search the ruler, period, conduct, and institutions named by the lens; include
primary/legal records, independent monitors or scholarship, reputable reporting, and
adverse or contrary interpretations. For a compound lens, vary searches across its
named mechanisms instead of treating one mechanism as the whole question. Record the
queries, promising candidates, opened sources, and rejection reasons. A lens may be
reported empty only after this pass finds no usable source-claim unit or a specific
access blocker is documented. Chapter-level source abundance and structured country
baselines do not substitute for this lens-level discovery check. Report both mapped
source-claim units and independent locator/source families. Seek at least three source
organizations and
two source types when the
available evidence permits, but do not manufacture diversity. Treat post-period material
as context unless it directly establishes a target-period fact. Empty or
`no_evidence_found` priors are gap signals and never retained evidence.

Do not assign scores, score ranges, anchors, ranking recommendations, or advice about
whether a judge should return a score or null. Do not use the client matrix. Your final
response is a flexible research handoff summarizing the global evidence register,
chapter yield and source diversity,
same-URL splits, cross-chapter reuse, rejected candidates, strongest contrary
evidence, and unresolved gaps. Self-audit temporal fit, ruler attribution, weak or
context-only items, and claims lacking independent corroboration. Include a local-data
audit stating which supplied fact IDs were retained, used as context, or left unused
and why. It does not need to match a JSON schema.

Execution requirement for tool-using models: do not finish on a planning statement,
an internal-thinking marker, or immediately after a search/page-inspection call. Begin
the research calls promptly; after every tool result either continue the chapter work
or synthesize the retained evidence. The final response must contain the substantive
selected-scope handoff itself, even when the notebook file was written successfully.
